# LumiRPEngine user guide

This guide is for two kinds of people: players, who want to chat in a world that remembers, and authors, who build those worlds. If you only want to play, read the first part and dip into the rest when you need it. If you are installing for the first time, start with the [README](../README.md).

- [How it works in one page](#how-it-works-in-one-page)
- **Playing:** [Play](#play) · [GM mode](#gm-mode) · [Swipes, edits and branches](#swipes-edits-and-branches) · [Time controls](#time-controls) · [Realism](#realism)
- **Tuning:** [Model use](#model-use) · [Character minds](#character-minds) · [Extraction modes](#extraction-modes) · [The Context inspector](#the-context-inspector)
- **Building:** [Authoring](#authoring)
- [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)

## How it works in one page

A **world** is a description of a place and the things in it: people, belongings, money, vehicles, locations, schedules, rules and quests. You attach a world to a chat, and the chat becomes a **world chat**. Everything the engine knows about the story so far is kept in two places: the world itself, and a small **record** saved on every AI reply that says what happened during that turn.

Every turn goes the same way:

1. **You act.** You type, tap an action in the Actions sheet, tap the map, or tap a chip under a reply.
2. **The engine runs first.** When you press Send (or Regenerate, swipe or Continue), Lumiverse starts the reply, and the engine stops it before anything is written: the status line says **Running the engine…**. The engine works out how much time passes, settles your action (its requirements, its dice check if it has one, its effects, how long it takes), and steps the world forward: NPCs go about their day, bills fall due, fuel burns. With minds on, each character present thinks first, while the engine reads your message.
3. **The engine writes the facts.** It writes the established facts as short lines in the world's own sections (Outcome, Scene, People and so on) and puts them in the world's lore book, where the prompt picks them up; then it starts the reply again by itself, with Lumiverse's own Regenerate or Continue. A new reply after a reply only you can start, so then the line says **Ready: press Send for the reply**.
4. **The narrator writes.** The AI writes the reply, with your action's outcome already settled. The engine's rules for it put your own instructions first (what you write for your character, and anything you say out of character), then what the story has already told, then the sections: the world as it stands, with its numbers, to follow unless you say otherwise. What the story brings in becomes part of the world. Under that order, they tell the narrator what the marks on facts your character doesn't know mean: every character stays consistent with what they know, and a marked fact isn't told to you in the reply unless you direct it or the story brings it out.
5. **The engine reads the reply.** It looks for changes the story implies (someone bought a coffee, moved to the kitchen, learned a public fact), checks each one against its limits, applies or asks about it, runs the people who are off screen, and saves the turn's record. The sections are emptied from the lore book once the reply ends. Your next turn's engine run waits for this reading to finish, since it starts from what the reading saved.

If anything the engine needed fails (reading your action, a character's mind, the time skip, or reading your settings as the turn starts), nothing is generated: your message gets a card saying what failed and why, with **Retry** and **Diagnostics**. A dropped connection or a busy provider is asked again twice on its own first. **Retry** asks only what failed: the minds that already answered are kept, as long as nothing they thought from changed. If the chat changes after the engine ran and before the reply starts (you edit your message, change one of the engine's settings, switch presets, or run Impersonate), what it prepared is never used stale: nothing is generated, and your message gets a card saying so, whose **Retry** runs the engine again and then writes the reply. Lumiverse saving its own settings (a drawer or a panel you moved) changes nothing the engine prepared.

With the chat open in two windows, only one starts the reply: the one you pressed Send in, or the first to open the chat. The other window's line says **Already started in another window**. If Lumiverse ran the reply for a window or client where the engine isn't running, the window showing the chat starts it instead. A window that was asleep or lost its connection when the engine finished starts the reply when you come back to it ("Ready; reopen the chat to continue"): it asks the engine again by itself when its connection is back, when the engine restarts, and when you return to it, so you don't need to reload the page. If the engine sends no view of the chat (building it failed, or nothing came within **Settings → Timeouts → Engine requests**), Play and GM say **No view of this chat from the engine** with why, and **Try again**.

On a Lumiverse too old to stop a generation, the chat, Settings and Diagnostics say **This Lumiverse can't stop generation; engine state may be missing**: replies then go out without the engine's sections.

**You and the story always win.** Your own instructions come first: what you type for your character, anything you say out of character (OOC), your ✎ fixes and GM edits, your settings and your answers on chips. Then comes what the story has already narrated. The engine's state comes last: it describes the world as it stands, and it follows the other two. With checks on, the dice decide an uncertain attempt, because you chose them. What each character knows works under that order: the narrator keeps every character, yours included, to what they know, and doesn't tell you in the reply a fact your character doesn't know, unless you direct it, in character or out of it, or the story brings it out through what is already established or what your character does or finds; GM mode is where you look behind the curtain. Because the engine keeps the numbers (prices, balances, rolls, the clock), they stay consistent however many times you swipe, edit or branch.

Where things live: the world's definition lives in LumiRPEngine's own storage, and your lore stays in your World Books, which work as usual. LumiRPEngine writes into your own entries only when you press for it in Build, and in a world chat it changes, for the prompt at hand only, just the secrets in them: a secret you mark in an entry's own text reaches the narrator marked as a secret (see [Your lore in world chats](#your-lore-in-world-chats)). The engine's helpers and minds read your World Info as lore too. The world adds one lore book of its own, named "LumiRPEngine · *your world*", which holds only the sections the engine fills in each turn. Records live on the replies themselves, so they follow swipes, branches, exports and deletions, and a backup journal in extension storage restores them if something overwrites them.

## Play

### The drawer

Open the **LumiRPEngine** tab in the Lumiverse drawer. The mode bar at the top moves between **Play**, **GM**, **Build** and **Settings**, and stays there in every mode. The mode you're in has a ring around it, gold in GM.

**Play** is the chat you're in. A bar along the bottom of the drawer holds six places:

| Place | What it holds |
|---|---|
| **Now** | The hub: where you are, what needs you, the fight while one is under way, and who is here. See [Now](#now). |
| **Me** | **You**, **Money**, **Garage** and **Gear**. See [Me](#me). |
| **People** | The people you know. See [People](#people). |
| **Orgs** | The organizations you belong to and know of, your party first. See [Orgs](#orgs). |
| **Places** | The map. See [The map](#the-map). |
| **Story** | **Quests**, **What you know**, **Journal** and **Calendar**. See [Story](#story). |

A place that holds more than one part shows them as tabs along its top. A place shows only while it has something to show: Now and Story always do, and a place whose packs the world doesn't use is left out. Everything follows your [realism](#realism) levels: a domain that is switched off has nothing to show, and a domain at a low level shows less. A dot on a place, or on one of its tabs, means detail is waiting there.

Details open as sheets inside the drawer: a person, a place, an item, an account or a quest. Each sheet has a back button at its top left. The back button or Escape goes back one level, and moving to another place, or opening another chat, closes them all.

If the chat isn't a world chat, Play says **Not a world chat** and lists **Your worlds**, each with an **Attach** button. Tap **Attach** and confirm in the **Attach world** box. **Open Build** takes you to the editors. A chat keeps the world it started with: attaching the same world again only re-attaches its lore book, and attaching a different one is refused with "This chat already plays another world. Start a new chat for this one."

The other modes: **GM** shows and edits everything, secrets included (see [GM mode](#gm-mode)), **Build** is where you make worlds (see [Authoring](#authoring)), and **Settings** holds your own defaults. Settings opens on a list of pages in groups: **Getting started** (**Helper model**, **Model use**, **Steps and connections**, **Minds**, **[Narrator](#what-the-narrator-is-fed)**, **Timeouts**), **Agents** (**[Agents](#settings--agents)**, **[Roles](#settings--agents)**, **[Main agent](#settings--agents)**, **[Assistant](#settings--agents)**, **[Prompts](#your-own-prompts)**, **[Retention](#retention)**), **Defaults for new chats** (**Realism**, **Checks**, **Time**), **Replies** (**Changes from the reply**, **Swipes**), **Your preset** (**Preset**), **World Info** (**[Lore](#settings--lore)**), **Display**, **Your agent** (**MCP server**) and **About** (**Caveats**, **[Diagnostics](#diagnostics)**). Tap a page to open it; its back button, or Escape, returns to the list. **Realism**, **Checks** and **Time** there set up chats you start from now on; the chat you have open has its own in GM mode, and each of those pages says so, with **Open GM → This chat** to go there. **Settings → Display → Show ids** is off by default. Turn it on to see the id the engine and the world's files use beside each name, in Build's lists, in Problems and in GM mode. Play never shows them: it names things only as your character knows them.

What you can see is always filtered to what your point-of-view character knows. Facts are public, known by particular characters, or secret, and a secret stays out of every place, sheet, chip, choice and Journal line until the story reveals it.

- **People.** Someone you can't see (a secret, or someone known only to particular characters whom you don't know) is never listed: not under **Here**, in **People** or as an action's target, even standing in the room, and the narrator isn't told they're there. You know the people you have dealt with (anyone you have a relation with, or whose things you hold) and anyone you're with. When someone leaves, People keeps what you saw: where and when you were last together, never where they are now or what they're up to.
- **Places.** Some places are hidden until you find them: they stay off the map, the travel list, the place fix and the narrator's facts until you have been there or hear of them. Once found, they stay found. A reply can tell you of one ("you hear about the old boathouse"): that's a **New facts** chip like any other. A secret place is never learned this way; the story reveals it.
- **What you've seen.** The engine keeps a record of every place you have been and of whom you last saw where, written as the story moves people around, so an existing chat builds it from its history.

### Now

**Now** is where Play opens. From the top:

1. **Banners.** **Paused** while the chat waits on an edit of yours, with the Keep report inside it (see [Editing an earlier message of yours](#editing-an-earlier-message-of-yours)), or **A turn is running** while a reply is being written: changes you make then apply when it finishes.
2. **The scene card**: the time and the part of the day (with the time scale when it isn't 1×, and **Clock paused** while the clock is paused), your place, the scene type with how long the last turn took, the weather and your cash. A value you can correct, the time among them, has an edit button (✎) beside it; see [Fixing a value](#fixing-a-value).
3. **Needs you**: the Keep report, then every decision under a reply that lapses when you send your next message, with how many there are. Tap one to see its reply: on a phone the drawer closes and the chat scrolls to it, and on a desktop the chat scrolls. If that reply isn't loaded in the chat, a notice asks you to scroll to it.
4. **The Encounter**, while a fight is under way: the round and whose turn it is, the turn order with HP and conditions, what you have left this turn, **Combat actions (n)**, which opens the Actions sheet on its **Combat** tab, **End turn**, and this round's acts. See [Combat and encounters](#combat-and-encounters).
5. **Here**: the people with you. Tap someone to open their person sheet.
6. **Glances**: short cards from the world's packs, each with **Open** to go to its part. **Coming up** shows your next two commitments, **Story** the next objective of the quest in hand, **Garage** low fuel, an overdue loan or service due, **Money** your money's alerts and then what's coming up within about three days (three rows at most, each opening the account or payment it names), **Gear** things you lent that are overdue, and **You** your conditions. A glance with nothing to say doesn't show.
7. **Quick actions**: **Act** opens the Actions sheet, **Timelapse** opens it on its Timelapse tab, and **Map** goes to **Places**.

### Me

**Me** has a tab for each part of your own life:

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **You** | Your name, where you are and what you're doing, and your tier; your conditions; then your character sheet in sections: stats, skills and derived values, as numbers or as words, depending on the level. A pool such as HP is a bar of current over max that turns red at a quarter or less, and a level shows a bar of XP toward the next one. A value you can correct has an edit button. |
| **Needs** | From Cast life 4: your hunger, fatigue, social and fun, each a gauge from 0 (met) to 100 (urgent), named in words ("hungry") once it's pressing, which is when the narrator hears of it too. Under each, the point it's told from ("From 60 you're hungry"), or that the narrator knows, and what meets it ("Met by eating"). A need the world switches off isn't shown. See [Needs](#needs). |
| **Money** | Your own money (see [Your money](#your-money)), under a row of segments, **Summary**, **Accounts**, **In & out**, **Assets** and **Credit**, each where you have something there, shown as simply or as fully as your Money level says. An organization's money is on its page in [Orgs](#orgs). In a new chat it shows the balances the world gives you to start with, under a **Not opened yet** banner, and the cash in the status line and the pill matches; opening the tab puts those amounts on your books, so the figures don't change when it does (during a reply they go on when the reply finishes). |
| **Garage** | A card for each vehicle: fuel (marked low when it is), condition, location and home base, odometer or engine hours, who has it on loan (with **Overdue**), and upcoming service. A location or home base you don't know about reads **Somewhere**. |
| **Gear** | What you have, by who has it rather than whose it is: **On you** (worn, in your pockets, in your hands and in your bags; the one that opens first), **Holding for others** (someone else's thing you have, with why), **Yours elsewhere** (with where it is), **Lent out** (with whom and until when, flagged **Overdue** once it should be back) and **Borrowed**; the empty sections but On you are left out. Containers nest what's in them with a load meter, and kits show what's missing ("Kit 3 of 5"). A container's chevron button opens and closes what's inside it. A container whose contents haven't been listed yet offers **Look inside**, which opens it (and, once shown, what's in it stays). Tap an item for its item sheet: where it is and what holds it, **Whose** it is, **Why you have it**, where it's **Kept at**, its slot and quantity, whom it is lent to and until when, its condition, a kit's pieces and what's missing, and a container's load and contents; and, where they apply, **Fetch**, **Put away**, **Give back** and **Hand to…**, each picked as the Actions sheet picks it. A thing of your own has **Not right?** with two ✎ corrections, out of the story: **Where is it?** (on you, in something of yours or that you may use, or at a place you know) and **Size**. Each gets a **Corrected** chip with its Undo. Make something Heavy or bigger and the next turn puts it down where it belongs. The engine refuses someone else's thing, a place you can't know of, and another person as the holder.<br>**Manage** (the switch at the top) shows the same things as **Outfit** (what you wear, by slot), **Carried** (what is on you and not worn, a bag's contents under the bag) and **Wardrobe** (your clothes at home, by wardrobe or dresser). Below Gear 3, everything is Carried. Each of your own things has the controls that change it at once, off screen: **Take off**, **Wear**, **Put in…**, **Put away**, **Drop** and **Take**. No message is sent, no turn plays and no time passes. Each change has its **Undo** in the notice above the list. Someone else's thing shows **Not yours** and has no controls. To do it in the story, use the palette or say it: palette taps still play as your next turn. |

### Your money

**Play → Me → Money** is your own money. What it shows follows your [realism](#realism): at Money 1 just a wealth band; from Money 2 your cash and accounts; from Money 3 cards, standing payments, income and bills; and at the top level (Money 4) the full books. Below the top level the books stay simple: balances, what's due, a few recent lines. The full books add each account's terms, statements, pending lines and holds, what your net worth is made of, cash flow by category and the next 30 days, investment lots with their gains, your paycheck's taxes and deductions, and your credit report. A money feature the world switches on (see [Realism](#realism)) shows its own part of these wherever it runs, and one it switches off shows nowhere, the top level included.

A row of segments sits under Me's tabs: **Summary**, **Accounts** (from Money 2), **In & out** (from Money 3), **Assets** (where investments or property run for you, or you hold a stake in an organization) and **Credit** (at Money 4), each only where you have something in it. With Summary alone, at Money 1, no row shows. A dot on a segment means something there wants you. A business you own or run, and your household, aren't here: their money, payroll, ownership and taxes are on their pages in [Orgs](#orgs), and what you hold there yourself (an account of yours, your share, your job) opens here as yours.

Each page opens over the drawer with a back button, and Escape goes back too. A segment's rows open their pages (an account, a payment, a job, a holding), and their controls open pages of their own, so nothing is more than three steps from its segment. Every control's page has you fill it in, then tap its one button, which says what it does (**Pay** *amount*, **Start the lease**, **Release**). While something is missing, or a box holds what it can't send (a 0 in **Every Nth time**, say), the button waits and the page says why just above it, even when that box is folded away under **More options**. Bookkeeping you do alone (buying or selling investments, exercising an option, where payouts go, moving money between your own accounts, paying a card or loan, autopay, freezing or replacing a card, standing payments, how you pay) happens at once, with no message, no reply and no time passing, and a chip naming what you did with **Undo** (GM → This chat → Edits lists it as your action, not a correction); one that can't happen says why, and nothing changes. Anything else (buying something, paying someone, cash at a machine, business and property) plays as your turn, and the story tells it.

**Summary.** A large figure leads: your net worth, with your wealth band under it (at Money 1, just the band), and a **Money** *n* badge that adds **· full books** when it shows them. Under it come **Cash on hand** and, in the full books or wherever investments or property run for you, **Assets** and **Owed**. Your first three alerts follow, each a banner with **Open** when it names an account or a standing payment, and **All alerts** opens the rest. **Coming up** lists what's due within the next seven days, each row opening the payment or account it names; then **Cash flow** for the last 30 days: **Money in** and **Money out**, by category in the full books, and **The next 30 days**.

**Accounts.** **Move money** leads, with a button for each way you may move money: **Transfer**, **Pay a card or loan**, **Cash out** and **Cash in**, each asking which account first. Then every account you hold or share, by kind (cash, bank accounts, cards and credit lines, loans, investment accounts), each row with its balance and what's due, or a loan's payment a month and the months left; the cards you hold on someone else's account (**Cards on others' accounts**); **Activity**, every line across your accounts, newest first, with an **Account** and a **Category** to narrow it to, and where holds run what's still **Pending** and the **Holds** on each account; and **How you pay**. Tap an account for its page: its balance, what's available, its limit (with how much of it is used), what's due, a loan's payment and months left, its interest, its owners when it's shared, its cards and its lines, each naming who it paid or who paid it (no one, for money between your own accounts). **Interest**, from Money 3, says what the account earns (savings and other bank accounts, at their APY, paid on the 1st of each month) or charges (cards and credit lines on a balance you didn't pay off, loans with each payment, at their APR) and how it's worked out, what it came to this year, the last it paid or charged, and about what the next comes to if nothing changes before then. A card charges nothing on a balance you pay in full, nor in its first month; where statements don't run (below Money 4), its statement day still ends each month's interest, on what's left unpaid of what it owed the month before, and cash drawn on it runs its cash-advance rate from the day you drew it. From Money 3, **Pay** pays what a card, a line or a loan owes, from the account you pick: the amount due, the whole balance, or another amount you type. **Transfer** moves money to another of your accounts, and **Withdraw cash** and **Deposit cash** move cash in and out of a bank account. At the top level, in a world that places bank branches or cash machines, that needs one where you are, and the page says so: a branch of the account's bank, or a cash machine. Taking cash out of a machine needs a card on the account (another bank's machine adds its fee), and paying cash in needs the bank's own machine that takes deposits, which holds the deposit for a few days. A world that places none leaves banking offstage, and cash moves anywhere. The **Autopay** row opens a page that sets how a credit account pays itself (the minimum due, the statement balance, the whole balance or a fixed amount, from an account you pick of its debtor's own money: theirs to spend, or theirs by its worth, so a custodial card pays from the custodial savings). **New standing payment**, on the page of an account you manage that can pay one, opens **New standing payment or one-off** with that account as **From**. A card opens its own page: its details, then **Change**, where you pick **Freeze**, **Unfreeze**, **Report lost** (it stops working at once) or **Replace**, each said in a line, and confirm with its button. **How you pay** lists your payment preferences, in order: a way of paying (cash, a debit, credit or charge card, checking, savings, or one card or account), for amounts and categories that fit; the first tier that fits and covers a purchase pays it, and if none does, your cash, debit card, credit card or charge card, whichever covers it, pays (savings, a money market or a trust account pays only when a tier names it or you do). Its **Change** button opens the page where you set them.

**In & out** is what comes in and goes out:

- **Income**: each job, as "Mechanic at Alder Automotive", with its next payday and what it last paid. A job's page shows its pay, schedule, next payday, gross pay each payday, the hours worked, the last paycheck, its **Payslip**, its **Deductions** (with taxes, in the full books) and **Pay goes to**. From Money 3, **Quit** leaves it.
- **Standing payments**: every standing transfer, bill and one-off of yours (not your pay, autopay or invoices, which have their own places), grouped **Active**, **Paused** and **Ended**; tap one for its page. **New standing payment or one-off**, at the top, sets one up: a name, from one of your accounts, to one of your accounts, someone you know or someone outside the world by name, an amount, and when: once on a day, or every day, week (on a weekday), month (on a day, or the last day) or year. Under **More options**: every second (or Nth) time, the time of day (09:00 when you don't say), an end date or a number of times, what happens on a weekend or a day-off holiday, a category, and what it does when the money isn't there: skip that time, pay what there is, try again later, pay from another account, go into overdraft as the account allows, or carry it to the next time. On a payment's page, **Edit**, **Pause**, **Resume** and **Delete** each open a page; an edit can rename it, and a time, end date, number of times or category you empty is gone. One that comes from a job, a lease or a loan says so on its page, and is changed there. Rent you pay under a lease also has **End the lease**, which gives notice: the lease ends today.
- **Invoices to pay**: the invoices billed to you, each opening **Pay now**, except where the rule below for money you only manage keeps a business's money from the sender's till (an invoice from a business of yours or your household's, or from one whose till you hold a card on, to a business you don't own outright); otherwise it's paid on its due date if the money is there.
- **Household**: your household's row, "Your share of the shared costs", which opens the household's page in Orgs, where its shared costs, who pays what and its shared accounts are (its **Money** tab).
- **Autopay**: each account that pays itself, opening its autopay page where you may change it.

**In & out** and **Activity** share one filter bar: **In** or **Out**, **Whose** (yours, your household's, a company or trust you run) and **Account**; Activity also filters by **Category**, and it lists every line, not only the newest. A correction (a ✎ cash fix, a GM money edit, cash you said you had) shows a **Correction** badge and never counts as money in or out.

**Assets** is what you own:

- **What it's made of** (the full books): each part of your net worth.
- **Investments**, where investments run for you: their worth, cost and gains, then each holding, **The market** and **Buy something new**. A holding's page shows its worth, cost and gain (and its lots in the full books), **Performance** (its price at each month's close and now as a line, and how it has done this month, this year and over 12 months, each once the market has closed the month it starts from with it), an option's strike and expiry, and **Distributions go to**, which says where its payouts go (reinvested, to an account, or to the usual place). Where investments run for you (from Money 4), the holding's page has **Buy more** and **Sell** (all of it when you give no amount), and **Buy something new** buys from what's on the market. A buy says which account pays for it and a sale where its money goes; by default, that's the account it's held in, else your usual account. Where options run, a buy can be a call or a put with its strike and expiry, and **Exercise** takes the contracts you choose (all of them by default) at the strike: a call's strike is paid from the account you pick, and a put's is paid into it. Where the world keeps the stock index's history (Modern US worlds do), **The market** shows the stock market at each of the last thirteen month-ends and now, how it has done this month, this year and over 12 months, and where its moves come from. From May 2003 to October 2026 the market moves as it really did, so its month-end closes are history's, the same in every chat; outside those months it moves at random with the swings it had then, the same in every chat of the world. A fund that tracks the market follows it closely; a single stock moves partly with it and partly on its own. A company listed in play is on the market too, by its name and ticker ("Ellis Mobile Repair (EMR)"), unless you don't know of it: you buy and sell it in whole units, only as many as its market has for sale (no options are written on it), and its holders share what the company pays out (see [Ownership and Taxes](#ownership-and-taxes)).
- **Property you own** and **For sale**. A property's page shows its value, your share, your equity, its mortgage, **Rent goes to** (which of your accounts the rent lands in), its leases and its running costs (tax, insurance, upkeep); one for sale that isn't yours shows only its listing: its value, where it is when you know the place, and its asking price. From Money 3, **Let it** starts a lease with a tenant you know, a lease row opens **End the lease** (the tenants of a managed property come and go on their own), and **Sell** sells it (its mortgage is paid off from the price first). A property for sale has **Buy**, paid from the account you pick, with a down payment; what the down payment leaves is a mortgage, over the term you pick under **Mortgage over**, from the terms the world's lenders offer (30, 20 or 15 years in the Modern US data, 30 years where a world sets none), with the usual one picked to start. The mortgage is a lender's, as if you had applied for it: it sets the rate by your credit, lends no more than the property is worth, and wants at least 3 % down; if it says no, nothing is bought. The mortgage pays the sellers, and what they owed on it is paid off first.
- **Stakes in organizations**: each business you hold a share of, with your role, your share and what it's worth, opening its **Ownership** tab in Orgs.
- **Your vehicles**, what they're worth together, opening the Garage.

**Credit**: your score with its band, on a meter from 300 to 850 (its history, what makes it and your report in the full books), and the credit you could apply for, each with its rate; an offer's row opens its **Apply** page.

A holding's **Held in** and a property's **Mortgage** open that account's page.

Every control here is something your character does, **done at once, off screen**: a tap does it now, with no message, no turn and no time passing, with **Undo** on its chip and **Tell** / **Don't tell** (whether the next turn's narrator hears it was done, "Off screen before this turn, you saw to this yourself: …"; payments to others, credit and property are told by default, your own accounts and investing aren't, as **Settings → Narrator → Out-of-play actions** sets). Type words to play it as a turn instead, and the reply narrates it. Buying something still plays as your turn, and cash goes hand to hand: handing it to someone who isn't where you are is refused. Your role on an account decides what you can do with it: an owner or a joint owner spends from it and manages it, a custodian or a trustee spends from it and manages it but its worth isn't theirs, a beneficiary sees it and counts its worth but can't spend it, and an authorized user spends with a card but manages nothing. If you own or run a business, you also manage its accounts by that role, from its **Money** tab in Orgs: you set up and change their standing payments, set their autopay and handle their cards, though you spend the business's money for yourself only with a role of your own on the account, such as an authorized user's card; to spend it for the business, pay as it (see [Acting for an organization](#acting-for-an-organization)). Money you only manage pays for whom it's for, never for you. That's a business's or a trust's money you hold no spending role on, and the business or trust paying as itself, whatever your role on it. A standing payment from it, or falling back on it, can't go to you, anyone in your household, a business or a trust one of you owns or benefits from (or one that those own in turn), or an account one of those counts as theirs, nor to someone outside typed as your name or your household's, in any capitals or spacing. Unless the money is wholly yours, it can't go into an account you can spend from yourself either (with an authorized user's card, or as its custodian or trustee), even one that's someone else's, unless that account is wholly theirs whom the money is for: as trustee of your daughter's trust and custodian of her account, you may pay her. A payment to someone by name is judged by the account it goes into, their deposit account, so paying someone whose only account you're custodian of is paying into that account; an invoice's payment is judged by the till of the business that sent it, and a worker's pay by their deposit account and their job's pay splits. It still pays whom it's for, such as a trust's beneficiary, even one in your household. Money that's wholly yours, a business you own outright, pays your other businesses and trusts too, though never you or anyone in your household: what you take out for yourself goes by a draw or a salary. The hub leaves out what it can't pay, and says so on the page when a name you type is yours or your household's. Working there gives you no say over its money, unless a position or a delegation gives you one (see [Powers](#powers)).

### People

**People** lists **Here** (the people with you) and then **People you know**. Each row shows their role, what they're doing or when and where you last saw them, how they feel about you, and any life state other than alive. **Filter and group** opens **Group by** (the world's classifications that players may see, a story role, say, or their groups or their tiers) and filters by those classifications' values and by group, with **Clear filters** (see [Classifications, groups and kinship](#classifications-groups-and-kinship)). **Your groups**, at the bottom, lists the groups and companies your character belongs to, each with the title you go by there and since when; a row opens that organization's page in [Orgs](#orgs).

Tap someone for their person sheet: where they are, or **Last seen** (from Cast life 2) with where and when you last saw them; their **Next** commitment; **Today**, a 24-hour timeline of where they will be, for someone who lives their own day, where **Why this plan?** gives the reasons for each block: a fixed commitment, the need pressing them ("hungry (hunger 62)"), a close call ("close call, Maddie chose"), or "a close friend"; **How they see you**, each axis in words; **What they're after**, their public agendas; their **Organizations**, each you can see with the title they go by there (and their position, where it differs), opening its page in Orgs; and **Known as**, the classifications you can see. Agendas show only when they're public, each on its own: time someone spends on an agenda that isn't public shows in their day as **Busy**, for "their own business", and someone working on one of their agendas is doing "agenda" only when all their agendas are public, else they are "busy" (here, in the Journal and to the narrator).

**Their usual day**, folded under **Today**, says where each block of that day comes from, as far as you may know: **Usually · their routine**, **Usually · from lore:** with the lore entry's title (only an entry you may read; a block resting on any other reads as their routine), **A commitment** (a shift, an appointment, a trip), **As the story has it**, **A plan of theirs** or **Free time**, with **Why?** for the reasons. It shows only for someone whose day Cast already shows, and never more than Cast does: a private block, or one spent on an agenda that isn't public, reads **Busy**, and no place or company shows. It's what they'd usually do, not a fact about what they're doing now: what happens in the story always comes first.

**Where are they?** on a person sheet corrects where they are, as **✎** on your own place (**Where you are**) does. Both open the place sheet: search by any word of a place's name or of the places around it, each result with its path ("Maple Falls › Apartment 4B › Bedroom"), or browse from the top level down, each row saying how many places it holds, with **Choose** to pick one. It offers only the places you may know of, and no path or level names a place you don't. Picking one moves them there for real (out of anything that held them, and what they were doing stays behind), and its **Corrected** chip has an **Undo**. Escape closes the sheet without changing anything.

### Orgs

**Orgs** (between People and Places) is every organization your character belongs to or knows of: your household, the company you work for or own, a group, a trust, and your party. Its page is **Organizations**, and it shows in every world chat, since your party is always there. What it shows follows your levels: members, positions, titles, powers, delegation and entry work at every Money level, Money 0 included, while an organization's money, payroll, ownership and taxes show at its own Money level, as yours do. Every control on its pages is, as Money's are, **done at once, off screen**: a tap does it now, with **Undo** on its chip and **Tell** / **Don't tell**; type words to play it as a turn instead (see [Your money](#your-money)).

**The home**, from the top:

1. **Your party**: its name (or "Your party"), who is in it ("Just you", or "Danny · 1 companion"), how many are here and away, and its **Purse** when it has one. It opens the party's page (see [The party](#the-party)).
2. **Where you belong**, grouped **Households**, **Companies**, **Groups** and **Trusts**: each with the title you go by there and the one figure you may see (a company's profit this month, a household's shared costs and your share, a group's members, a trust's net worth). "You don't belong to any organization yet." when there is none.
3. **Known organizations**, folded, with how many: the ones you know of without belonging, each with whom you know there.
4. **Start something…**: **Start a company** (its name, the capital you put in and the account it comes from: from Money 3, you its only owner, with a business checking account of its own), **Start a group** (its name and one of the world's group kinds: you lead it, with every power over it) and **Name your party**. Founding plays as your turn.

**An organization's page** has Back, its name, a badge for what it is ("Company · LLC", "Household", "Group · Faction", "Trust", "Party") and your title there. Its **⋯** holds **Rename** where you may govern it (at once, with Undo), **Leave** where you belong by a link of your own (as your turn; an owner leaves a company by selling their share), and **Open in GM**, which opens it in GM → Orgs. A row of tabs follows, with only those it has and you may see:

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Overview** | Its headline ("Mechanic since 12 Jan 2026; Marcus Delgado runs it") and its figure; **Your place here** (your title, your position and whom it reports to, since when); **What you may do**, family by family, each with what it rests on (an owner's role, a position, a delegation, a member's default); **Delegations you hold**, with their limits and until when; and its **History**, the latest five lines with who did each and when, the rest under **More**. A line about its money shows only to whoever may see its money. |
| **People** | **Hire someone** and **Admit a member** at the top, where you may; then each position with its seats ("1 of 1", or "2 held" where any number may hold it) and whom it reports to, its holders under it or **Vacant**; then its staff and members without a position and its owners with their share (the heading opens **Ownership**); and "And 2 others you don't know" for anyone you can't see. A row shows a name, a title and **Here** or **Away**, and opens their sheet (below). A company's **Payroll** closes the tab: how many are on it, what it has cost this year, where its unemployment taxes run (Money 4) **FUTA this year** and **SUTA this year**, each with its rate on its wage base ("Federal unemployment tax: 0.6% on the first $7,000.00 of each worker's pay"), and each job, which opens its page with **Raise** and **Let go** where you run it. |
| **Plan** | Its goals and the orders given for them, with their statuses (see [The Plan](#the-plan)). |
| **Money** | Its own money, in the same segments as yours (a company's **Books** among them: its profit this year, month by month, and its sales), read as you may see it: **Money** needs a power that sees its money (`money.view`). What it shares with you (an account you hold or run, your job) opens as your own page, with your controls; its invoices offer you **Void** and **Pay** as your role allows, and **Issue an invoice** where you run it. **Pay as…** leads where you may spend its money (see [Acting for an organization](#acting-for-an-organization)). |
| **Property** | Its premises and who may enter them (see [Entry and Property](#entry-and-property)). |
| **Ownership** | A company's owners, its shares and structure, and what you may decide (see [Ownership and Taxes](#ownership-and-taxes)). |
| **Taxes** | How its income is taxed, its exempt status and **Donate**, a consolidated return, its unemployment taxes, what falls due next, and its taxes this year and last (see [Ownership and Taxes](#ownership-and-taxes)). |
| **Decisions** | Its motions put to a vote, its requests for approval, and how its decisions are made (see [Decisions: votes and approvals](#decisions-votes-and-approvals)). |
| **Rules** | Its positions, delegations and dues (below); the party's are its tiers. |

#### People and their sheets

A person's **sheet** shows their positions, title, rank and since when; how they belong (member, owner, staff); **What they may do**, each power with what it rests on; **Delegations they hold**; and **Their job** (pay, hours, how often they're paid and the next payday, and whether they're working or on leave) where you may see its money. Under **What you may do** come the controls you may use on them, each by a power you hold there:

- **Appoint…** puts them in one of its positions or takes them out of theirs, within the position's seats (`members.appoint`); **Change title…** gives them a title of their own, or yours (`members.appoint`, or your own);
- **Delegate…** and **Revoke…** (see [Delegation](#delegation));
- for someone who works for it, from Money 3: **Raise**, **Change hours**, **Put on leave** or **Back from leave** (`staff.pay`), and **Let go** (`staff.fire`), never on yourself;
- **Remove**, for a member by a link of their own (`members.remove`).

**Hire someone** (`staff.hire`, from Money 3) asks whom (anyone you know who doesn't work there), their title, a position, how they're paid and the rate, their hours a week and how often they're paid (left out, its pay schedule's); any employer hires this way, a household or the party as much as a company. **Unpaid** is for a title nobody is paid for (a director, a trustee): no pay and no payslip; a raise later starts pay from that day. **Admit a member** (`members.admit`) asks whom, a title and a position (left out, the one new members join in). Each control is your character's act, done at once, off screen, with Undo (anything done to or with a person — hiring, admitting, appointing, a title, pay, hours, leave, letting go, removing, delegating — is told to the next turn's narrator unless you choose **Don't tell**; type words to play it as a turn instead). The engine judges each again in its own words: a position whose seats are full, someone outside your reach, or someone whose pay would land in money you run for others is refused with why. **Change pay schedule**, in the Actions sheet (`staff.pay`), moves every job there to one schedule at once.

On a trust's page, its trustees and beneficiaries are grouped and titled by role (**Trustees**, **Beneficiaries**), and a role that carries no worth shows no share. A trust pays its beneficiaries a stated amount, by their shares, from its account: **Distribute profit** with an amount (refused without one), recorded as "Trust distribution".

#### Rules: positions, delegation and dues

**Positions** lists each one with its powers in words ("Every power", or family by family), its seats, whom it reports to, how far its staff and member powers reach (the whole organization, or only the positions below it), its limits ("$500 a payment"), who fills it when it falls vacant (the next in line, someone named, or nobody), and badges: **New members** for the one new members join in, **Built in** for a type's own (a household's Head, the party's Leader). Where you may govern it (`org.govern`), **Add position** and each position's **Edit** open its editor: **Title**, **Powers** as chips by family, **Seats** (0 is any number), **Reports to**, **Its staff and member powers reach**, **Each payment up to** and **Each month up to**, **When it falls vacant** and **New members join in it**. A position's change applies at once, with Undo.

**Delegations** lists who acts for it, with what powers and limits, until when and from whom (see [Delegation](#delegation)). **Dues**, for a household or a group from Money 3, is what each member pays into its money and how often; **Set dues** (`org.govern`) sets **Each member pays**, **How often** (each week, month or year) and the day, at once.

#### Powers

What anyone may do for an organization is one of its 21 **powers**, in seven families:

| Family | Powers: they may… |
|---|---|
| **Members** | admit members; remove members; appoint people to positions and give titles |
| **Staff** | hire staff; let staff go; set staff pay, hours, schedules and leave |
| **Money** | see its money; spend its money; manage its accounts, standing payments, autopay and cards; send and void its invoices; pay out its profit; borrow in its name |
| **Things** | use its things; lend and assign its things; sell, give away or use up its things |
| **Property** | manage its property: leases, rent, entry and keys; buy and sell its property |
| **Ownership** | issue, buy back and register its shares |
| **Governance** | set its positions, rules and structure; give orders; delegate its powers |

Each money power lets its holder see the money it reaches, and lending or disposing of a thing lets them use it. A power rests on one of four things, the first that gives it: an **owner's role** (a company's or a trust's managing owner, joint owner, custodian or trustee holds every power; any other owner sees its money), a **position** they hold, a **delegation** to them, or, failing those, what the type gives a **member** by default: a household's or a group's members see its money and use its things, and lend or use up what is consumed in use; the party's core see its money and use its things, and its companions use its things. Staff, officers, hired hands and allies get nothing by default. A household's **Head** (a member holding its Head position, else the member titled head, the most senior first) and the party's **Leader** (you) hold every power.

A position or a delegation may carry **limits**: so much a payment, so much a calendar month, only certain accounts. An act over one is refused in its words ("Over your limit of $500.00 a payment for Alder Automotive"), unless you ask for approval, which puts it up your chain of command instead (see [Decisions: votes and approvals](#decisions-votes-and-approvals)). A position that reaches only **the positions below it** hires, appoints, pays and lets go only those below it. And money you only manage pays for the organization, never for you: its money doesn't pay you, your household or anything of yours ("Alder Automotive's money pays for Alder Automotive, not for Jordan Ellis").

#### Delegation

Someone who holds `delegate` may let another person act for the organization with some of their own powers: **Delegate…** on a person's sheet, or on **Rules**, picks the powers (only ones you hold), **Each payment up to**, **Each month up to** and **Until** a date (left empty, until it is revoked). A limit of yours stands on the delegation unless you give a lower one, and a reach confined to those below you can't be given for the whole organization. It ends at the end of its **Until** day. **Revoke…** ends it: its granter, or anyone else who holds `delegate` and every power it gives. Delegating and revoking play as your turn. The delegate's Overview lists it under **Delegations you hold**: its powers, until when ("Until it is revoked" when it has no end), from whom and its limits.

#### Acting for an organization

Where you may spend for an organization, by any basis, **Pay** and **Buy** in the Actions sheet offer **Paying as**: **You**, then each organization you may spend for. Pick one, and its money pays, its line in the books names you ("Paid by Jordan Ellis for Alder Automotive"), and what you spent counts toward your monthly limit there; the Go button ends with whom you pay as (", as Alder Automotive"). A thing you buy for an organization is its, and lands with you. Changing the action puts it back to You. Typing it works too: "pay Danny $20 from the shop's till" is read as paying as the shop, where you may spend for it.

An organization's **Money** tab has **Pay as…** at the top for whoever may spend its money there: it asks whom (the people Pay offers) and how much, says the limit your power there carries ("From its money, up to $500.00 a payment"), and **Pay** *amount* **as** *it* pays it at once. Over the limit it says so and offers **Ask for approval to pay** *amount* instead, also at once: nothing is paid until someone above you approves it (see [Decisions: votes and approvals](#decisions-votes-and-approvals)). Its Activity shows who made each line for it.

Other money actions take an organization the same way, by the power each needs: moving money, a card's changes, autopay, standing payments, trading and where payouts go by `money.manage`; applying for credit by `money.borrow`; buying and selling property by `property.trade`, and leases by `property.manage`; sending and voiding invoices by `money.invoice`; paying out profit by `money.payout`; selling a stake by `shares.manage`.

#### The party

Your **party** is the people who travel with you, each in a tier:

| Tier | What it means |
|---|---|
| **Core** | Comes along wherever you go. Sees the party's money and uses its things. |
| **Companion** | Comes along wherever you go, unless their own shift, appointment or plan is due. Uses the party's things. |
| **Hired** | Works for the party for pay. Comes along only when you take them. |
| **Ally** | On your side, but goes their own way: comes along only when you take them. |

The narrator hears the tier ("Danny Okafor (party companion) is here."), and "we" in what you type means your party here. The party's page lists its members by tier (**Core**, **Companions**, **Hired**, **Allies**), each with where they are as you know it and since when. As its leader you have **Add someone you know** (with a tier), **Change a tier** (or **Take out of the party**), **Name the party** (later **Rename the party**), **Start a purse** (from Money 2: an account the party owns, opened with your deposit from an account of yours; core members see it), **Share out** once it has one (an amount paid equally to its core members and companions, never you, hired hands or allies) and **Hire a hand** (from Money 3, once it has a purse: a job the party employs, paid from the purse, and the hired tier). Each is greyed with why while you may not. The roster, the name and the purse are bookkeeping, at once with Undo; hiring and sharing out play as your turn. When a hired hand's job with the party ends, the page asks: **Leave the party**, or **Stay as a companion**. Until you name it or start a purse, the party has no entity of its own and nothing is the party's. Its **Rules** tab says what each tier means.

#### Entry and Property

A place is someone's: the business that trades there, else whoever lives there, else the organization that holds it. Who may enter it follows the same ladder a thing's use does: **Owner only**, **Members only** (a person's household, or an organization's members), **Staff only**, **Customers** or **Anyone**, each admitting those before it. Whoever lives there may always enter, and so may anyone carrying one of its keys. Left unset, a business's place admits customers while it trades and only its staff outside its hours, and anywhere else admits anyone; a room takes its house's entry.

**Go** to a place that turns you away is greyed with why ("Private: Marcus Delgado's", "Members only", "Staff only"), and so is the map's **Walk to**. The story still wins: if the reply walks you in, you are there, and the place's facts tell the narrator "You're in Marcus Delgado's house without leave." unless someone there may let you in. Only a move the story tells counts; a routine, a schedule or a GM's move never does. The Place section also says whose a place is ("12 Birch Lane is Marcus Delgado's."), when you know.

An organization's **Property** tab, where it holds places, has **Premises and entry**: a card for each place with **Who may enter** (and where that's set, when a place around it sets it), **Now**, its **Hours**, its **Keys** and who carries one, and, for whoever may change them, the latest entries without leave. **Change entry** (`property.manage`) picks who may enter, or **As the world has it**, and applies at once with Undo; the card then says **Changed in play**.

#### Ownership and Taxes

A company's **Ownership** tab, for whoever may see its money, opens with its structure ("LLC"), whether it counts its shares in units ("1000 units", or "not counted in units") and what its decisions need ("decisions need more than half of the votes"), and your own part of the votes when you hold any. Then:

- **Cap table**: each owner with their role, and where it counts units their class, their units, their votes where those differ from their share, their share of its worth and, at Money 4, what their shares are worth (**shares worth** …). An owner you don't know reads "an owner". A listed company's cap table goes on with its holders **on the market**: each one who bought its stock in the world ("an investor" where you don't know them), then **The public** for the rest of its float.
- **Worth**: two figures, kept apart. From Money 3, **Net worth**: what it holds less what it owes, the figure its Money tab's Summary shows, which goes below zero (in red) when it owes more than it holds. At Money 4, **Value of the shares**: set for it, or worked out from its net worth and a multiple of the last 12 months' profit, and in units **A unit**, that value over its units. A listed company's is the market's, its price a unit times all its units, with its net worth beside it. The value of the shares never goes below $0: its owners aren't liable for its debts, so a company whose net worth is below zero says so ("Net worth is below zero: …") and its shares are worth nothing, while its net worth shows how far under it is.
- **Market**: a listed company's **Ticker** and when it listed, its **Price a unit** and how it has moved this month, its **Market capitalization**, what **The public holds** (how many of those units are held in the world and how many the market still sells) and, at Money 4, **Its books a unit**, the level its price returns to. Each trading day its price moves around its books a unit: the stock market (its history, where the world keeps it) moves it, partly or, as Build sets, wholly, and as its books grow or shrink its price follows. Then, each folded and, where you may not use it, off with why:
  - **List on the market**, for a C corporation (change its structure first): the **New units for the public**, the **Price a unit** (empty, its books a unit) and the **Ticker** (empty, its initials). The public pays no more for them than they're worth, its books a unit, unless Build lets it pay more ("the public pays no more than its books a unit for new units of Ellis Mobile Repair, $2.00 a unit"). The market pays for them into its account, its owners keep their units, and everyone's share is now of all its units, the public's among them. Once it's listed, **Offer more units** sells more the same way (empty price: its market price, which is also the most the public pays), and it stays a C corporation until it's taken off the market.
  - **Sell on the market**: some of your own units at its market price, paid into your usual account; they join the public's, and you realise any gain. A listed company keeps an owner on its cap table, so your last units sell only to someone in the world.
  - **Take it off the market**: it buys back every public unit at its market price (or more, if you say), from its account; whoever holds its stock in the world is paid and realises their gain, and its owners hold all of it again.

  The public's units vote too, so a holder of 40% of all its units holds 40% of the votes; holders on the market hold no powers there. When it pays out profit, the public's part goes to its holders by their units: to each one in the world, and the rest to the market.
- **Structure**, where another company owns it or it owns one: who owns it above ("Owns it, and controls it" for one with more than half its votes), this company, and its subsidiaries below, each with its share, whether it controls it and, at Money 4, its net worth, the value of its shares (a trust's net worth alone), cash and profit, theirs indented under them, four deep. At Money 4 a company that controls any adds **Cash, with its subsidiaries**, **Profit this year, with them** and **Net worth with its subsidiaries**, which counts each one it controls at its net worth rather than the value of its shares (a value set for one, or the market's, stays), so a subsidiary under water pulls it below its own net worth; money moved between them, such as the capital that started one, changes none of these.
- **Payouts**: **Pay out profit** and **Owner's draw** (an owner or a joint owner, never from a C corporation) where you may, and **Your share of its profit goes to**, where your part of what it pays out lands.
- **Shares**: **Sell shares**, for your own stake: in units where it counts them, else in percent, at a price for the whole (nothing gives it away) to someone you know, or all of a company you own outright to a buyer outside the world. Then the decisions, each folded and, where you may not use it, off with why:
  - **Issue shares**: to whom, how many units, the **Price a unit** (empty, its value a unit) and, where it has classes, which. The buyer pays into its account, and every owner's share is diluted. A company that doesn't count units yet counts its owners in them first: 1,000, unless the world sets its own in Build.
  - **Buy back**, where it counts units: whose units, how many and the price a unit; the company pays them from its account, and they realise any gain.
  - **Classes**: a class by name with its **Votes a unit** (0 makes it non-voting), and an owner's units to move into it.
  - **Start a subsidiary**: its name, its capital, from the company's own money (never a subsidiary's), and, if you like, its units. The company owns all of it.
  - **Change structure**: one of the five. Draws and payouts change at once, its taxes from the next estimated-tax period.
  - **Merge**: another company you decide for (**With**), **Which way** (**Fold it in here**, fold this one into it, or **Take it over** as a subsidiary that keeps its staff and accounts), the units of the one that goes on for each unit of the other (empty, by their values a unit) and any **Cash to its holders**, paid from the account of the one that goes on. Folded in, the other company first pays what it owes its suppliers and in taxes (a corporation's income tax still owed among them); then its accounts, property, staff, positions, invoices and the standing payments it makes or takes become this one's (a client's payment to it lands here), its owners get the units and cash, and it is dissolved. You need the votes in both companies.

  Every one is done at once, off screen. A decision needs more than its threshold of the votes on your own, or, deciding as an organization that holds its shares, on that organization's: "Issuing shares in Ellis Mobile Repair needs more than half the votes; Jordan Ellis holds 40%". Short of them, **Issue shares**, **Buy back**, **Classes**, **Change structure**, **Wind up**, **Merge**, **List on the market** (or **Offer more units**) and **Take it off the market** offer **Put it to a vote** instead: it is done if the vote passes (see [Decisions: votes and approvals](#decisions-votes-and-approvals)).
- On each subsidiary under Structure, where you act for the company, **sell part, or put more in**: sell part of its stake, or issue it more units of one it controls, as the company. A company's people hold no powers in its subsidiaries, so this is how it runs them.
- **Wind up**, where you may decide for it: what winding it up would do now: its **Cash, with what it would sell**, what it would pay of what it owes (wages due, invoices billed to it, taxes, loans and cards; the unemployment tax its last paychecks add among them, and a corporation's **Income tax still owed** for each part of the year no payment has covered), what it would write off of what it is owed, **What it holds** and where each goes, the **Staff who would go**, the **Standing payments that would end**, and each owner's payout by their share. Anything it holds, or a standing payment, that you don't know of reads unnamed ("A holding", "A property", "A stake in another company", "Something it owns", "A standing payment"). Wind it up **All at once**, or **Begin winding up**: it stops trading (its staff go, its standing payments end, it takes no more walk-in custom and hires, bills and borrows no more) while you sell what you like as the company, then **Finish and dissolve** or **Resume trading**. **Its property** (real estate and stakes) is handed to its owners by share or sold at its value; its things and vehicles go to one owner (**Its things go to**, else its largest owner). Its cash is gathered into an account of its own (never one it shares or only uses), and the rest of it goes to its owners by share, each realising any gain against what they put in. A company that owes more than it holds reads "It is $… short" and can't be wound up until you sell something or put money in. Winding up is done at once and needs the votes, like the decisions above: "Winding up Ellis Mobile Repair needs more than half the votes; Danny Okafor holds 20%".

A company that is winding up shows "Winding up since …" at the top of its page; one that is dissolved shows "Dissolved on …" (with **Open** for the company it merged into), keeps no controls, and reads "Dissolved …" in Orgs. Its History stays with the people it names.

The **Taxes** tab (a company's and a tax-exempt organization's from Money 3; any other organization that employs, at Money 4) shows, in this order, what applies:

- **Tax-exempt**, for an organization the GM made exempt: **Exempt as** its status ("Church or religious organization", "IRC 501(c)(3): it pays no income tax"), **Donations to it** (**Deductible**, a person's gifts coming off their income tax, for a charity or a church; **Not deductible** for a social club) and **Unemployment taxes it owes** (**SUTA only** for a charity, **Neither** for a church, **FUTA and SUTA** for a club).
- **Donate**, on an exempt organization: an amount, then **Donate $25.00**, which pays it as you pay, at once. A gift to a charity or a church is deductible: at Money 4 it comes off your taxable income (on top of the standard deduction, up to $1,000, or $2,000 married filing jointly; more when the gifts are more, up to 60% of your income), and what that saves you is refunded each 15 April.
- **Income tax**: **It pays corporate income tax**, with its tax on this year's profit so far, for a C corporation (on its own profit and what the pass-through companies it owns pass up to it); **Filed on a consolidated return** with its parent's name, for one a parent's return includes; **Exempt from income tax** for an exempt organization; else **Taxed to its owners**: its profit is their income, through any companies between, in their estimated tax, and each person at the top is listed with their share. An organization at the top that pays no income tax (a household, a group, a trust) reads **no one pays tax on this share**, as Problems warns in Build; an exempt one reads **exempt: its exemption keeps this share untaxed**. A change of structure that is waiting says when it applies ("Taxed as a C corporation from 1 Jan 2027."), and once a company is taxed as a corporation its owners' estimated tax no longer counts its profit.
- **Consolidated return**, for a C corporation that holds 80% or more of another, by votes and by worth (through others too): **It may file one consolidated return** with the corporations it would include, each with the share of its votes and of its worth, and **File a consolidated return**, done at once where you govern it (else off, with why). Once filed, **It files one consolidated return**: their profits and losses offset, **The group's profit this year** and **The group's tax so far**, which it pays for all of them (each state still taxes each on its own), and **Stop filing one**. A corporation wound up or merged away pays the income tax it still owes as it goes: one its parent's return files pays its own on its own profit, and a parent pays the group's, which counts as each member's own tax paid for it.
- **Unemployment taxes**, for an employer where they run (Money 4, or from 3 where the world switches them on): **Federal unemployment tax (FUTA)** at its rate after the credit for state tax ("0.6%", "6% less a 5.4% credit for its state tax; on the first $7,000.00 of each worker's pay a year"; a credit-reduction state's reduction is due with the annual return) and **State unemployment tax (SUTA)** at its state's rate on its state's wage base ("2.7%", "on the first $9,500.00 of each worker's pay a year"); **None** for one its exempt status spares. Where they start partway through a year (the world takes the Data update, or the money level rises), each worker's wage base counts what the employer already paid them that year.
- **Due next**: its sales tax, its corporate income tax, its FUTA and SUTA (paid on the last day of January, April, July and October; FUTA under $500 carries to the next quarter) and its share of property tax, each with what it comes to and when.
- **This year** and **Last year**: sales tax collected, remitted and held, what it withheld from its staff's pay and its own share of payroll tax, its FUTA and SUTA and what it has paid of each, the income tax it paid, its property tax and the donations it received.

#### Decisions: votes and approvals

Some decisions are more than one person may make alone. An organization's **Decisions** tab, for its voters, anyone in one of its motions or requests, and whoever may see its money or govern it, holds them:

- **To a vote**: each open motion, with what it would do, **Put by**, **Closes**, **Votes** (for, against and abstaining, and whether enough took part), each voter with their part of the votes and their vote, **Your vote** (**For**, **Against** or **Abstain**, and **Vote as** an organization whose shares you manage), cast at once, and **Withdraw** for whoever put it.
- **Waiting for approval**: each request, with what it would do, **Asked by** and the limit it is over, **May approve**, **Lapses**, and **Approve** or **Deny** (with **Why (if you deny it)**) for whoever may approve it, each done at once; **Withdraw** for whoever asked. A request, waiting or decided, shows only to whoever asked it, may approve it or answered it, and to whoever may see the organization's money or govern it: voting alone (holding a listed company's stock, say) shows none.
- **Decided**: the latest closed: a motion **Carried**, **Failed** with why ("no quorum: 40% took part, 50% needed", "voted down: 40% for, 60% against") or **Withdrawn**; a request **Approved**, **Denied**, **Withdrawn** or **Lapsed**, with who answered.
- **How decisions are made**: **Votes are counted by** **Shares** (each unit its class's votes, else each owner's share of its worth; a company's way unless set) or **Members** (one each; any other organization's), **Quorum** (the votes that take part, 50% unless set), **Passes with more than** (of the votes cast for or against, 50%), **A motion stays open** (7 days) and **A request waits** (7 days), and the standing votes; where you may govern it (`org.govern`) you edit them, at once with Undo. Where you vote, **Standing vote** (**When you don't vote yourself**: For, Against, Abstain, or **With…** another voter) is cast for you as a motion opens, or as that voter votes; your own vote replaces it.

**Motions.** Where your votes alone fall short, **Put it to a vote** (in Ownership) opens a motion at once with your vote for it, nothing done yet. Who votes, and how much each counts, is fixed as it opens. It carries the moment the votes for it are enough that the rest can't stop it (with enough taking part), and fails the moment they can no longer carry it; else, when it closes, it carries on the votes cast or fails for want of them, or of a quorum. Carried, it is done in that same moment, as if whoever put it had done it, and History says it carried; one that can't be done any more (the money is gone) fails, saying why. Motions of different organizations closing at the same moment are done one after another, each with what the one before left.

**Requests for approval.** An act for an organization over your limit (each payment, the month, its accounts), asked for approval (**Ask for approval to pay** in Pay as…, or **Ask for approval when it's over your limit** on Pay, Buy, Move money and Share out), goes up your chain of command only: whoever granted the delegation your power rests on, then whoever holds the positions above yours, then whoever governs or owns the organization, nearest first, and each only within their own limits. Someone else there, however high their own limit, may not approve it; and where no one above you may, it is refused as before ("…, and no one above you may approve it"). Nothing moves while it waits. Approved, it is done then as yours (your name on its line, counted to your month), and History names who approved it; denied or withdrawn, it closes; unanswered after its days, it lapses.

**The story and the GM.** No one votes or answers on their own: the narrator is told the open motions and requests you are in, a character's mind those it votes on or may approve, and a vote or an answer the reply tells ("Danny raises his hand for the issue", "Marcus signs off on the order") is read as **Votes on organizations' motions** or **Answers to requests for approval**, which ask you first (see [Extraction modes](#extraction-modes)). In **GM → Orgs** the GM votes for anyone, closes a motion on the votes cast or withdraws it, and approves or denies any request.

#### The Plan

An organization's **Plan** tab (any organization but a trust, and your party) holds its **goals** and the **orders** given for them: who each order is for and who leads, the task in the words it was given, where, and by when. An order with no time is a **standing order**. Each shows its status (**Open**, **Under way**, **Blocked**; closed as **Done**, **Dropped** or **Refused**), who set it, when and from where ("Set by you at 9:40 AM, from your message"), and the latest word on it. **Late** marks one whose time has passed: the clock never closes anything, so it stays on the plan until someone does. **Yours** marks one asked of you, and **Should be done** one that, by the day as laid off screen (below), should be finished though nobody has said so.

Goals come first, each with its orders under it, then the orders that serve no goal, then **Closed**, the latest ten, each with **Reopen**. Where you may give orders there (`orders.give`; in your party, you, its leader) you have **Add a goal** (its words, **By when**: today or a date, and where), **Give an order** (to any of its people you can see, who leads, where, when and the goal it serves), and on each item **Edit**, **Done**, **Drop** and **Reopen**, plus **Mark plan done** once every order of a goal is done. Anyone an order is asked of moves their own along with **Under way**, **Done**, **Blocked** and **Refuse**, but never drops it. Each applies at once, with Undo; in GM → Orgs the GM edits the same board. Someone who doesn't belong there and holds no power there sees none of it. Your party's roster shows each member's order ("On: Sweep the bays"), and Now's **Organizations** glance gives the party's plan in a line ("Plan: 3 of 7 done · Tyler away").

**From the story.** Orders and goals you give in your message ("Danny, you're on the brake job for the Civic tonight") go onto your party's plan, or onto an organization's you name and may give orders for, each in its own words and only with words your message holds: the reply shows **Added to the plan: 1 goal, 2 orders** with **Edit**, which opens that organization's Plan, and **Undo**, or **Not added to the plan:** and why. The reply moves items along (under way, done, blocked, refused) when it shows them so, quoting itself, but never adds one: when someone in it volunteers ("I'll handle the permits"), a chip asks **Add to the plan? Danny Okafor: Handle the permits**, and a yes gives them that order from you.

**Who is told.** The narrator gets each plan you're on in the Quest section ("Your plans and the orders given"), with who set what and when, and is told to show progress only as your message and the time that passes cover, never to mark anything done or hand out a task itself. Each person an order names reads it in their mind first (see [What minds and the narrator are told](#what-minds-and-the-narrator-are-told)).

**Off screen**, at Cast life 3 and up, your party's orders shape its members' days, whether or not they have minds:

- An order with a place or a time is laid as an hour's work in their day, between 6 AM and 10 PM, as soon as it fits: from the minute after you gave it until its time, or for a standing order with a place, within its goal's window (else within a day). It weighs by their tier (core and hired most, then companions, then allies), so a core member's order can take the place of their shift. Someone who lives a day of their own goes and does it, and their day plan reads "On an order: Sweep the bays".
- While a timed order is **under way**, their routine gives way to it until its time, as to the GM's intent: they stay at it.
- A standing order **under way** holds them to it through each day's waking hours until its goal's window ends; with no window, until it is done or dropped, around their own shifts.
- A member on an order bound to another place stays behind when you go elsewhere; a hired hand on an order with you on its team ("with me") comes along.
- Once everything laid for an order has passed, it reads **Should be done**, and the narrator hears "By the day as laid it should be done; not told yet". That closes nothing: you, the reply or the GM still decide. Changing the order's time, place or people clears it.
- In a time skip, the model's plan for the skip hears each order in your words, and it plans the skip of any member on an order (unless this chat set how they spend skips). The Montage's first group, **Orders**, says who worked on what ("Danny Okafor worked on: Sweep the bays").
- An order still on the plan counts as pending with you, so its person's mind comes up more readily, and those who live a day of their own are caught up first after the people in your scene.

Below Cast life 3, orders steer only the narrator and the minds. A blocked order lays nothing.

#### Alerts and Coming up

An organization's **Overview** opens with its **alerts**, worked out as you look, each with **Open** to the tab it concerns:

- a vacant seat ("Master is vacant.");
- payroll short two paydays running ("Your pay here is two paydays behind." when it's yours);
- a delegation ending within 3 days ("Your delegation to act for it ends …" when it's yours);
- dues in arrears ("Your dues are $20.00 behind." when they're yours);
- negative equity two months running;
- a motion open for your vote (**Needs you** until you vote), and a request for approval waiting on you, or yours waiting.

An alert that asks something of you (you hold the power to act on it, or it's your own) is marked **Needs you**, and the organization's row on the Orgs home carries a dot. Now's **Organizations** glance lists up to three of them, then your party's plan. Money's alerts follow the organization's Money level, as its Money tab does.

**Coming up**, above its History, lists the next two weeks (the world's orgs settings may say otherwise): paydays, invoices due in and to pay, dues and lease ends, each with when and, where you may see its money, how much. Where you only work or pay dues there, it lists your own payday and dues.

#### Succession

When someone holding a position stops acting (they die, or leave the world), the next slot fills their seat, but only where the position names who comes next (**When it falls vacant**, on its Rules editor): **the next in line** is the most senior holder of a position reporting to it, and **someone named** is that person, if they're still acting. They take the seat, and a title the holder went by with it passes too ("Captain Rupert Thorne succeeds Sir Alaric Thorne as Master" in its History, and a notice when you belong there). Nothing else passes: shares and things move only by the story or the GM. Succession is on unless the world turns it off (Build → Packs → orgs); **GM → This chat** can switch it on or off for this chat alone, which wins over the world's.

#### What minds and the narrator are told

A character's mind reads **Your organizations** (on by default, 200 tokens: [Settings → Minds](#settings--minds) → What a mind reads): first what was asked of them on any plan, with who asked, the team, where and by when, how it stands and the goal it serves, and how their tier takes it (a core member treats it as binding and may object, then does it; a companion weighs it; a hired hand gets it done as the job; an ally may agree or not); a core member also reads the rest of the party's plan.
